Fact 3.35
.

The functions
\mathsf{check}
and
\mathsf{DNF}
commute. To be precise, let

•

\phi
be a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Delta_{0}
formula, and

•

\nu
be a
\mathfrak{A}
-valuation.

Then
\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{DNF}(\phi,\nu))
and
\mathsf{DNF}(\mathsf{check}(\phi)
are logically equivalent. As such, without loss of generality, we can assume

\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{DNF}(\phi,\nu))=\mathsf{DNF}(\mathsf{check}(\phi),\nu)\text{.}

(3.2)

In fact, (
3.2
) already holds if
\phi\in ran(\mathsf{WNF})
.

Lemma 3.36
.

Let

•

\phi=\ulcorner\bigwedge\mathrm{S}\wedge\bigwedge\mathrm{T}\urcorner
for some

\circ

S
is a finite subset of
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{0,\mathfrak{A}}
, and

\circ

T
is a finite subset of
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
containing only formulas either of the form
\ulcorner E(x)\urcorner
or of the form
\ulcorner\neg E(x)\urcorner
,

•

\nu
be a
\mathfrak{A}
-valuation, and

•

\Sigma\subset\mathcal{L}
in some weak outer model of
V
.

If
\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi))
, then
\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi))
is
\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}
-nice for
\Sigma
.

Proof.

By the form of
\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi))
, it is sufficient to prove that whenever

•

\Sigma^{\prime}\supset\Sigma
,

•

\Sigma^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice, and

•

\varphi
is a subformula of
\nu^{*}(\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi)))
of the form
\ulcorner E(x)\urcorner
,

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi\iff\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi
.

That
\Sigma\subset\Sigma^{\prime}
means

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi\implies\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi,

so it is sufficient to prove

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner\implies\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner.

We examine the possible cases below.

Case 1:

x\not\in\mathcal{L}
. Then
x\not\in\Sigma^{\prime}
, so
\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner
.

Case 2:

x\in\mathcal{L}
and
\varphi
occurs in
\mathsf{pos}(\phi)
. Then
x
cannot be a variable symbol. Since
\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi))
logically implies
\mathsf{pos}(\phi)
and

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi)),

also

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}\mathsf{pos}(\phi).

By the definition of
\mathsf{pos}
,
\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner
must not occur in
\mathsf{pos}(\phi)
, so
\varphi
is a conjunct of
\mathsf{pos}(\phi)
, and
\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi
. As a result,

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner\implies\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner

trivially holds.

Case 3:

x\in\mathcal{L}
,
\varphi
does not occur in
\mathsf{pos}(\phi)
and moreover,
\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner
does not occur in
\nu^{*}(\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi)))
.

If
\varphi
occurs in
\nu^{*}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi))
then by the same argument as in Case 2,
\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi
and we have our desired conclusion. Otherwise,
\varphi
occurs as a subformula of
\nu^{*}(\varphi^{\prime})
for some
\varphi^{\prime}
of the form

\ulcorner(\text{``}x\in\mathcal{L}\text{''}\implies\text{``}E(\neg x)\text{''})\urcorner\text{,}

where
x
is a variable. By the fact that

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi)),

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner
means
x\not\in\mathcal{L}
, in which case also
\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner
.

Case 4:

x\in\mathcal{L}
,
\varphi
does not occur in
\mathsf{pos}(\phi)
and moreover,
\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner
occurs in

\nu^{*}(\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi)))
.

Here
\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner
must occur in
\nu^{*}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi))
, so by the definition of
\mathsf{check}
,

\ulcorner(\text{``}x\in\mathcal{L}\text{''}\implies E(\neg x))\urcorner

occurs in
\nu^{*}(\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi)))
. Now

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi))

implies either
x\not\in\mathcal{L}
or
\neg x\in\Sigma
.

Subcase 1:

x\not\in\mathcal{L}
. Then clearly
x\not\in\Sigma^{\prime}
, so
\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner
.

Subcase 2:

\neg x\in\Sigma
. Then
\neg x\in\Sigma^{\prime}
because
\Sigma\subset\Sigma^{\prime}
. Since
\Sigma^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice, it must be that
x\not\in\Sigma^{\prime}
. We thus also have
\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg\varphi\urcorner
. ∎

Remark 3.37
.

It will be useful to note that if
\varphi
is a member of
\mathcal{D}
, then every disjunct
\phi
of
\varphi
takes the form required of
\phi
in (the hypothesis of) Lemma
3.36
.

3.2
Forcing Notions and Universal Sentences

Consider a forcing notion with conditions fragments of
\mathcal{L}
, ordered by reverse inclusion. Then genericity over
\mathbb{P}
naturally gives us a subset of
\mathcal{L}
. We want to analyse this subset using
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
.

Definition 3.38
.

A pair
(\mathfrak{A}=(A;\in,\vec{R}),\mathbb{P}=(P,\leq_{P}))
is
good for

\mathcal{L}
iff

•

\mathfrak{A}
is
\mathcal{L}
-suitable,

•

\emptyset\neq P\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{L})\cap A
, and

•

for all
\{p,q\}\subset P
,
p\leq_{P}q
iff
q\subset p
.

For the rest of this subsection, we fix a forcing notion
\mathbb{P}=(P,\leq_{P}))
for which

•

\mathbb{P}
is
\Sigma_{1}
-definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
, and

•

(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P})
is good for
\mathcal{L}
.

Similar in motivation to the final bullet point in Definition
3.4
, the requirement for
\mathbb{P}
to be
\Sigma_{1}
-definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
is only there so that the proof of Lemma
3.42
can go through given its hypothesis.

Definition 3.39
.

For
p\in P
, a
p
-candidate for

(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universality
is a set
\Sigma
for which

•

p\subset\Sigma
,

•

for each
x\in[\Sigma]^{<\omega}
, there is
q\in P
with
p\cup x\subset q
, and

•

\Sigma
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice.

Definition 3.40
.

Let
\phi\in\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
be a sentence and
p\in P
. We say
\phi
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
iff for all
q\leq_{P}p
there is a set
\Sigma
in some weak outer model of
V
such that

•

\Sigma
is a
q
-candidate for
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universality, and

•

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi
.

We say
\phi
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal
iff for all
p\in P
,
\phi
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal
p
.

For
\Gamma\subset\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
, we say
\Gamma
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal
(
for
p
) iff
\phi
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal (for
p
) for all
\phi\in\Gamma
.

For notational convenience in the proofs to follow, we introduce the following definition.

Definition 3.41
.

For
p\in P
, let
\mathcal{F}_{p}
denote the set

\{\Sigma:\Sigma\text{ is a }p\text{-candidate for }(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})\text{-universality in some weak outer model of }V\}.

Lemma 3.42
.

Let

•

W
be a weak outer model of
V
,

•

g\in W
be a
\mathbb{P}
-
\Sigma_{1}
-generic filter over
\mathfrak{A}
,

•

p\in g
, and

•

\phi
be a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentence which is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
.

Then
\bigcup g
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice and
\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi
.

Proof.

We prove
\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi
by induction on the length of
\phi
. The proof that
\bigcup g
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice will surface as a part of the induction argument.

Case 1:

\phi
is
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Delta_{0}
. By way of contradiction, assume
\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\neg\phi
. Since
\neg\phi
is also a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Delta_{0}
sentence, Lemma
3.32
tells us there are finite sets
p^{\dagger}\in A\cap\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{L})
and
q^{\dagger}\in A\cap\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{L})
such that

•

\bigcup g\cap q^{\dagger}=p^{\dagger}
, and

•

\neg\phi
is
\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-true for
(p^{\dagger},q^{\dagger})
.

For each
z\in p^{\dagger}\subset\bigcup g
, pick a
p_{z}\in g
such that
z\in p_{z}
. Since
g
is a filter, there is some
p^{*}\in g
for which

p^{\dagger}\subset(\bigcup_{z\in p^{\dagger}}p_{z})\cup p\subset p^{*}.

If
p^{\prime}\leq_{P}p^{*}
, then
p^{\prime}\leq_{P}p
, so by
\phi
being
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
, there is
\Sigma\in\mathcal{F}_{p^{\prime}}
with
\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi
. Now, necessarily

•

p^{\dagger}\subset\Sigma
and

•

\Sigma\cap q^{\dagger}\neq p^{\dagger}
,

whence
p^{\dagger}\subsetneq\Sigma\cap q^{\dagger}
. By the fact that
\Sigma
is a
p^{\prime}
-candidate for
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universality, we can find
q\in P
with

p^{\prime}\cup(\Sigma\cap q^{\dagger})\subset q.

As a consequence,
q\leq_{P}p^{\prime}
and
p^{\dagger}\subsetneq q\cap q^{\dagger}
.

We have thus shown that the set

D_{1}:=\{q\in P:p^{\dagger}\subsetneq q\cap q^{\dagger}\}

is dense below
p^{*}
in
\mathbb{P}
. Given the fact that
\mathbb{P}
is
\Sigma_{1}
-definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
,
D_{1}
obviously has the same property, so
g
must meet
D_{1}
. As
p^{*}\in g
, we can conclude
g\cap D_{1}\neq\emptyset
, and let
q^{*}\in g\cap D_{1}
. But then
p^{\dagger}\subsetneq q^{*}\cap q^{\dagger}
, which implies

p^{\dagger}\subsetneq q^{*}\cap q^{\dagger}\subset\bigcup g\cap q^{\dagger}=p^{\dagger},

a contradiction.

Case 2:

\phi
is
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Sigma_{1}
but not
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Delta_{0}
. Then
\phi
is of the form
\ulcorner\exists x_{1}\dots\exists x_{n}\ \phi^{*}\urcorner
for some

•

\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Delta_{0}
formula
\phi^{*}
,

•

n
such that
1\leq n<\omega
, and

•

\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\}
the set of free variables of
\phi^{*}
.

We first show that
\bigcup g
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice. Obviously,
\bigcup g\subset\mathcal{L}
, so we need only consider the other two conditions of being
\mathcal{L}
-nice. To that end, define

\varphi_{x}:=\ulcorner(E(x)\vee E(\neg x))\wedge(\neg E(x)\vee\neg E(\neg x))\urcorner

for each
x\in\mathcal{L}
. Note that the
\varphi_{x}
’s are
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Delta_{0}
sentences. Moreover,
X\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi_{x}
for every
\mathcal{L}
-nice set
X
and every
x\in\mathcal{L}
.

Let
x\in\mathcal{L}
and
p^{\prime}\leq_{P}p
. That
\phi
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
means
\mathcal{F}_{p^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
, so choose any
\Sigma\in\mathcal{F}_{p^{\prime}}
. We must have
\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi_{x}
because
\Sigma
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice. This allows us to conclude that for all
x\in\mathcal{L}
,
\varphi_{x}
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
. As Case 1 has been proven, we can apply it to yield

\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi_{x}\text{ for all }x\in\mathcal{L},

which is just another way of saying
\bigcup g
fulfils the last two conditions of Definition
3.7
. We have thus shown that
\bigcup g
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice.

Once more, let
p^{\prime}\leq_{P}p
, so that there is
\Sigma\in\mathcal{F}_{p^{\prime}}
for which
\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi
. By Remark
3.25
, we can safely assume
\phi^{*}\in ran(\mathsf{WNF})
. Then there is
\nu
such that

•

\nu
is a
\mathfrak{A}
-valuation, and

•

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}\phi^{*}
.

According to
(2)
of Proposition
3.28
, it must be the case that

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}\phi^{\prime}\text{,}

(3.3)

where

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}:=\mathsf{DNF}(\phi^{*},\nu)\text{.}

Define two other
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
sentences as follows:

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime\prime}:=\

\displaystyle\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi^{*}))

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}:=\

\displaystyle\ulcorner\bigvee\{\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\psi)):\psi\text{ is a disjunct of }\phi^{\prime}\}\urcorner\text{.}

Clearly,

\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}=\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\phi^{\prime}))\text{,}

from which, citing Proposition
3.27
and Fact
3.35
, we can conclude

\phi^{\prime\prime\prime}=\mathsf{DNF}(\phi^{\prime\prime},\nu)\text{.}

(3.4)

Finally, set

\phi^{\dagger}:=\ulcorner\exists x_{1}\dots\exists x_{n}\ \phi^{\prime\prime}\urcorner\text{.}

It is imperative to highlight that
\phi^{\dagger}
depends on
\phi^{*}
, and thus on
\phi
, but not on any
\mathfrak{A}
-valuation.

Remark 3.43
.

Note that we have just described a constructive procedure which converts an arbitrary
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Sigma_{1}
sentence
\phi
into another
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Sigma_{1}
sentence
\phi^{\dagger}
. Indeed, this procedure makes sense even if
n=0
in the expansion of
\phi
, i.e. even if
\phi\in\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Delta_{0}
. From now on, call the function associated with this procedure
\mathsf{Conv}
, so that both the domain and codomain of
\mathsf{Conv}
are equal to

\{\phi:\phi\text{ is a }\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\text{-}\Sigma_{1}\text{ sentence}\}\text{.}

Claim 3.44
.

There exists
q\in P
for which

•

q\leq_{P}p^{\prime}
, and

•

q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

Beginning with (
3.3
), we observe that there ought to be some
\varphi
for which

•

\varphi
is a disjunct of
\phi^{\prime}
, and

•

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\nu^{*}(\varphi)
.

\Sigma
being
\mathcal{L}
-nice and Lemma
3.12
gives us
\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{pos}(\nu^{*}(\varphi))
.
Since literals of the form

\ulcorner\neg E(x)\urcorner\text{ for some }x\in\mathcal{L}

do not occur in
\mathsf{pos}(\nu^{*}(\varphi))
, following the proof of Case 1 of Lemma
3.32
, there must be a finite set
p^{\dagger}\in A\cap\mathcal{P}(\Sigma)
such that

•

\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{pos}(\nu^{*}(\varphi)))=(p^{\dagger},p^{\dagger})
, and

•

\mathsf{pos}(\nu^{*}(\varphi))
is
\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-true for
p^{\dagger}
.

By the fact that
\Sigma
is a
p^{\prime}
-candidate for
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universality, we can find
q\in P
with

p^{\prime}\cup p^{\dagger}\subset q.

In particular,
q\leq_{P}p^{\prime}
and
q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{pos}(\nu^{*}(\varphi))
.

That
\phi
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
tells us that
q
can be extended to a
\mathcal{L}
-nice set. Necessarily,
q
must be “internally consistent” in the following sense:

\displaystyle q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg E(x)\urcorner\text{ whenever }

\displaystyle x\in\mathcal{L}\text{ and }

\displaystyle\ulcorner\neg E(x)\urcorner\text{ occurs in }\nu^{*}(\varphi).

If
x\in\mathcal{L}
and
\ulcorner\neg E(x)\urcorner
occurs in
\nu^{*}(\mathsf{pos}(\varphi))
, then
\ulcorner\neg E(x)\urcorner
already occurs in
\nu^{*}(\varphi)
. Consequently,
q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\ulcorner\neg E(x)\urcorner
. We have thus established

q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\nu^{*}(\mathsf{pos}(\varphi)).

(3.5)

Let
\varphi^{\prime}
be of the form

\ulcorner(\text{``}x\in\mathcal{L}\text{''}\implies\text{``}E(\neg x)\text{''})\urcorner\text{,}

where
x
is a variable such that
\ulcorner\neg E(x)\urcorner
occurs in
\mathsf{pos}(\varphi)
. Then the fact that
q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{pos}(\nu^{*}(\varphi))
must imply
q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\nu^{*}(\varphi^{\prime})
. Bearing the definition of
\mathsf{check}
in mind, this allows us to ascertain that

q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\nu^{*}(\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\varphi)))

follows from (
3.5
). As
\nu^{*}(\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\varphi)))
logically implies
\nu^{*}(\phi^{\prime\prime\prime})
, we have
q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\nu^{*}(\phi^{\prime\prime\prime})
as well. Invoking (
3.4
) and
(3)
of Proposition
3.28
now would yield
q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}\phi^{\prime\prime}
, from which

q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi^{\dagger}

logically follows.
∎

Claim 3.45
.

For every pair
(\Sigma^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime\prime})
in any weak outer model of
V
such that

•

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice, and

•

\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
,

we must observe

\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi^{\dagger}\implies\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi\text{.}

Proof.

Assume

•

(\Sigma^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime\prime})
is in some weak outer model of
V
,

•

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice,

•

\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
, and

•

\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi^{\dagger}
.

By
(2)
of Proposition
3.28
, this means we can find
\nu^{\prime}
and
\varphi^{*}
such that

•

\nu^{\prime}
is a
\mathfrak{A}
-valuation,

•

\varphi^{*}
is a disjunct of
\mathsf{DNF}(\phi^{\prime\prime},\nu^{\prime})
, and

•

\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu^{\prime}}\varphi^{*}
.

By an argument similar to that which led us to (
3.4
),
\varphi^{*}
must be a disjunct of

\ulcorner\bigvee\{\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\psi)):\psi\text{ is a disjunct of }\mathsf{DNF}(\phi^{*},\nu^{\prime})\}\urcorner\text{,}

and so

\varphi^{*}=\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\psi))

for some disjunct
\psi
of
\mathsf{DNF}(\phi^{*},\nu^{\prime})
. Seeing that
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice and recalling Remark
3.37
, we are permitted to apply Lemma
3.36
to obtain

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu^{\prime}}\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\psi)),

or equivalently,

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\nu^{\prime})^{*}(\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\psi))).

According to the definition of
\mathsf{check}
in Definition
3.33
, it is immediate that
(\nu^{\prime})^{*}(\mathsf{check}(\mathsf{pos}(\psi)))
logically implies
(\nu^{\prime})^{*}(\mathsf{pos}(\psi))
, so we also have

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\nu^{\prime})^{*}(\mathsf{pos}(\psi)).

By Lemma
3.12
and the fact that
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice,

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\nu^{\prime})^{*}(\psi).

That
(\nu^{\prime})^{*}(\psi)
logically implies
(\nu^{\prime})^{*}(\mathsf{DNF}(\phi^{*},\nu^{\prime}))
then yields

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu^{\prime}}\mathsf{DNF}(\phi^{*},\nu^{\prime}).

Now we can invoke
(3)
of Proposition
3.28
to arrive at

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu^{\prime}}\phi^{*}\text{,}

from which

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi

logically follows.
∎

Claim
3.44
informs us that the set

D_{2}:=\{q\in P:q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi^{\dagger}\}

is dense below
p
in
\mathbb{P}
. With reference to
(I)
and Remarks
3.11
and
3.34
, as well as Definition
2.7
for what it means to be a
\Sigma_{1}
formula,
D_{2}
is
\Sigma_{1}
-definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
, since

•

\mathbb{P}
is
\Sigma_{1}
-definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
,

•

we may safely assume
\phi^{\dagger}
to be
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Sigma_{1}
,

•

the act of replacing every subformula of
\phi^{\dagger}
of the form
\ulcorner E(x)\urcorner
with
\ulcorner x\in q\urcorner
does not alter the quantification structure of
\phi^{\dagger}
,

•

\mathfrak{A}
being a model of a sufficiently strong set theory means that for each

\circ

\Sigma_{1}
formula
\varphi^{+}
, and

\circ

pair of variables
\{\ulcorner z\urcorner,\ulcorner p\urcorner\}
with both
\ulcorner z\urcorner
and
\ulcorner p\urcorner
not being bound in
\varphi^{+}
,

there exists a
\Sigma_{1}
formula
\phi^{+}
satisfying

\displaystyle X\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}\ulcorner\forall z\ (z\in p\implies\varphi^{+})\urcorner\iff X\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A},\nu}\phi^{+}

in case

\circ

X
is a set in some weak outer model of
V
, and

\circ

\nu
is a
\mathfrak{A}
-valuation,

and thus,

•

one can assume without loss of generality, that the result of replacing every subformula of
\phi^{\dagger}
of the form
\ulcorner E(x)\urcorner
with
\ulcorner x\in q\urcorner
, is a
\Sigma_{1}
formula in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
.

Thus
g
must meet
D_{2}
. As
p\in g
,
g\cap D_{2}\neq\emptyset
. Choose any
q^{*}\in g\cap D_{2}
. Obviously,

•

q^{*}\subset\bigcup g
, and

•

(q^{*},\bigcup g)\in V[g]
.

We have also shown that
\bigcup g
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice. As a result, the pair
(q^{*},\bigcup g)
satisfies the hypothesis of Claim
3.45
. Applying Claim
3.45
to
(q^{*},\bigcup g)
then gives us

q^{*}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi^{\dagger}\implies\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi\text{.}

Now
q^{*}\in D_{2}
just means

q^{*}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi^{\dagger},

so necessarily,

\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi\text{.}

Case 3:

\phi=\ulcorner\forall x\ \varphi(x,\vec{d})\urcorner
for some
x
,
\varphi
and
\vec{d}
. Then for each
a\in A
,
\varphi(a,\vec{d})
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
. By the induction hypothesis,

\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi(a,\vec{d})

for all
a\in A
, so also

\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi.

∎

The upshot of Remark
3.43
and Claims
3.44
and
3.45
, given the choice of parameters therein, is the general fact below.

Fact 3.46
.

Let

•

\phi
be a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Sigma_{1}
formula, and

•

\Sigma
be a
\mathcal{L}
-nice set in some weak outer model of
V
.

Assume
\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi
. Then

(1)

for every pair
(\Sigma^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime\prime})
in any weak outer model of
V
such that
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice and
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
,

\Sigma^{\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{Conv}(\phi)\implies\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi\text{,}

and

(2)

for every
p\in P
with
\Sigma\in\mathcal{F}_{p}
, we can find
q\in P
such that

(a)

q\leq_{P}p
, and

(b)

q\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\mathsf{Conv}(\phi)
.

Remark 3.47
.

Let
p
be an arbitrary member of
P
and define

\mathbb{P}_{\leq p}:=(\{q\in P:q\leq_{\mathbb{P}}p\},\leq_{\mathbb{P}}).

Then

(1)

\mathbb{P}_{\leq p}
is
\Sigma_{1}
-definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
,

(2)

(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P}_{\leq p})
is good for
\mathcal{L}
,

(3)

any
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentence which is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
is also
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P}_{\leq p},\mathcal{L})
-universal, and

(4)

whenever
g
is a
\mathbb{P}
-
\Sigma_{1}
-generic filter over
\mathfrak{A}
containing
p
,

g\cap\{q\in P:q\leq_{\mathbb{P}}p\}

is a
\mathbb{P}_{\leq p}
-
\Sigma_{1}
-generic filter over
\mathfrak{A}
.

Consequently, Lemma
3.42
is equivalent to, and can be restated as:

Lemma 3.29
′
.

Let

•

W
be a weak outer model of
V
,

•

g\in W
be a
\mathbb{P}
-
\Sigma_{1}
-generic filter over
\mathfrak{A}
, and

•

\phi
be a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentence which is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal.

Then
\bigcup g
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice and
\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi
.

Remark 3.48
.

The proof of Lemma
3.42
can be reused to prove the following variation of said lemma.

Lemma 3.49
.

Assume

•

\mathcal{L}
is just definable (instead of
\Pi_{1}
-definable) in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
, and

•

\mathbb{P}
is just definable (instead of
\Sigma_{1}
-definable) in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
.

Let

•

W
be a weak outer model of
V
,

•

g\in W
be a
\mathbb{P}
-generic filter over
\mathfrak{A}
,

•

p\in g
, and

•

\phi
be a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentence which is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
.

Then
\bigcup g
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice and
\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi
.

By recycling the argument in Remark
3.47
with

•

“definable” in place of “
\Sigma_{1}
-definable”,

•

“
\mathbb{P}
-generic” in place of “
\mathbb{P}
-
\Sigma_{1}
-generic”, and

•

“
\mathbb{P}_{\leq_{p}}
-generic” in place of “
\mathbb{P}_{\leq_{p}}
-
\Sigma_{1}
-generic”,

we can conclude that Lemma
3.49
is equivalent to, and can be restated as:

Lemma 3.32
′
.

Assume

•

\mathcal{L}
is just definable (instead of
\Pi_{1}
-definable) in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
, and

•

\mathbb{P}
is just definable (instead of
\Sigma_{1}
-definable) in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
.

Let

•

W
be a weak outer model of
V
,

•

g\in W
be a
\mathbb{P}
-generic filter over
\mathfrak{A}
, and

•

\phi
be a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentence which is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal.

Then
\bigcup g
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice and
\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi
.

Remark 3.50
.

The astute reader may notice that Case 3 of the inductive step in the inductive proof of Lemma
3.42
seems insubstantial and tagged on. Indeed, removing the need to consider said case in Lemma
3.42
does not weaken the lemma. More generally and more precisely, substituting “
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Sigma_{1}
” for “
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
” in any of

•

Lemma
3.42
,

•

Lemma
3.29′
,

•

Lemma
3.49
, and

•

Lemma
3.32′

always returns a statement of equivalent strength.

As a display of reciprocity, Lemma
3.42
allows us to simplify our verification procedures for the universality of certain
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentences. The proof of this next lemma is spiritually similar to (the most obvious) proofs of the four equivalences highlighted in Remark
3.50
.

Lemma 3.51
.

Let

•

p\in P
, and

•

\phi(x)
be a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
formula with
x
as its only free variable.

Suppose for each
a\in A
,
\phi(a)
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
. Then

\varphi:=\ulcorner\forall x\ \phi(x)\urcorner

is a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentence
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
.

Proof.

Clearly
\varphi
is a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentence. Fix
q\leq_{P}p
. It suffices to find a set
\Sigma
fulfilling

•

\Sigma
is a
q
-candidate for
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universality, and

•

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi
.

Choose any
\mathbb{P}
-
\Sigma_{1}
-generic filter
g
over
\mathfrak{A}
from amongst the weak outer models of
V
, such that
q\in g
. Note that for each
a\in A
,
\phi(a)
is a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentence
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
q
, since
\phi(a)
is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
and
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universality is inherited downwards in
\mathbb{P}
. By Lemma
3.42
,

\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi(a)\text{ for all }a\in A\text{.}

But this just means

\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\varphi\text{.}

We know
\bigcup g
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice due to Lemma
3.42
. That
\bigcup g
is a
q
-candidate for
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universality then follows from the following facts:

•

q\in g
, and

•

g
is a filter.

All in all, we have shown that
\bigcup g
is the
\Sigma
we are looking for.
∎

A natural strengthening of Lemma
3.42
is to have
\phi
be an arbitrary
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{3}
sentence which is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal for
p
. As per Case 3 in the proof of Lemma
3.42
, we can always get the outermost universal quantification for free, so we only have to prove the strengthened lemma assuming
\phi
is
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Sigma_{2}
instead of
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{3}
. However, the nice “characterisation” of
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentences we will uncover in Section
5
(brought about by Theorems
5.36
and
5.34
) seems to suggest that such a strengthening is impossible. With Remark
3.47
in mind, it makes sense to ask the following question.

(Q0)

Are there sets
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}=(P^{\prime},\leq_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}})
,
W
,
g
, and
\phi
such that

•

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is closed under negation,

•

\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
is
\Sigma_{1}
-definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
,

•

(\mathfrak{A}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime})
is good for
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
,

•

W
is a weak outer model of
V
,

•

g\in W
is a
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-
\Sigma_{1}
-generic filter over
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
,

•

\phi
is a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
-
\Sigma_{2}
sentence which is
(\mathfrak{A}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
-universal, and

•

\bigcup g\not\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}\phi
?

We shall defer answering
(Q0)
until the next subsection.

3.3
A Useful Framework

Fix a set of
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentences,
\Gamma
, for this subsection.

In the previous subsection, we saw how a forcing notion
\mathbb{P}
can generate witnesses to certain
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentences when
\mathbb{P}
is definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
and
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P})
is good for
\mathcal{L}
. Leveraging on this fact, we shall develop a framework for defining forcing notions that generate witnesses to a given set of
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentences.

This framework both generalises and is inspired by the forcing construction Asperó and Schindler carried out in the proof of the main theorem of
[
16
]
.

Definition 3.52
.

A set
B
is
\mathcal{L}
-closed under finite extensions
iff

•

B\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{L})
, and

•

for all
x\in B
and all
y\in[\mathcal{L}]^{<\omega}
,
x\cup y\in B
.

Definition 3.53
.

For any
\Sigma
and any
p
, we say
\Sigma

\Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies

p
iff

1.

p\subset\Sigma
,

2.

\Sigma
is
\mathcal{L}
-nice, and

3.

\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\Gamma
.

It is easy to see that if
\mathcal{L}
,
\mathfrak{A}
,
\Gamma
,
\Sigma
and
p
are such that
\Sigma

\Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
p
, then

•

\Sigma

\Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
q
for all
q\subset\Sigma
, and

•

\Sigma

\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
p
for all
\Gamma^{\prime}\subset\Gamma
.

This gives us the following proposition.

Proposition 3.54
.

Let
|\mathcal{L}|\leq\lambda
and
B
be
\mathcal{L}
-closed under finite extensions. If we define
\mathbb{P}:=(P,\leq_{\mathbb{P}})
, where

\displaystyle P:=\

\displaystyle\{p\in B:\ \Vdash_{Col(\omega,\lambda)}\exists\Sigma\ (``\Sigma\ \Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }p")\},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\leq_{\mathbb{P}}\ :=\

\displaystyle\{(p,q)\in P\times P:q\subset p\},

then as long as
P\neq\emptyset
,

•

(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P})
is good for
\mathcal{L}
, and

•

whenever
p\in P
and
\Sigma

\Gamma^{\prime}(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
p
for some
\Gamma^{\prime}\supset\Gamma
,
\Sigma
is a
p
-candidate for
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universality.

Lemma 3.55
.

Let
|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|\leq\lambda
and
p\subset\mathcal{L}
. Assume there is
\Sigma
in a weak outer model
W
of
V
such that
\Sigma

\Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
p
. Then

\Vdash_{Col(\omega,\lambda)}\exists\Sigma\ (``\Sigma\ \Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }p").

Proof.

Suppose otherwise, so there is
q\in Col(\omega,\lambda)
such that

\displaystyle q\Vdash_{Col(\omega,\lambda)}\ \neg\psi,

(3.6)

where

\psi:=\exists\Sigma\ (``\Sigma\ \Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }p").

Let
g
be
Col(\omega,\lambda)
-generic over
W
with
q\in g
, so that
g
is also
Col(\omega,\lambda)
-generic over
V
. First, that
W\models\psi
means
W[g]\models\psi
. Next, notice that if
\varphi(\Sigma,y)
is the conjunction of the statements

•

y=\{\phi\in\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}:\Sigma\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\phi\}
,

•

\Gamma\subset y
,

•

p\subset\Sigma
, and

•

``\Sigma\text{ is }\mathcal{L}\text{-nice}"
,

then
\varphi
is a
\Sigma_{1}
(in fact,
\Delta_{1}
, although that delineation is unnecessary here) formula in the language of set theory (see Definition
2.7
), with parameters among
p,\mathfrak{A},\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}},\Gamma
. This is because
\varphi
is equivalent to the statement of there being a function
f
with domain
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
— a
\Delta_{0}
-definable subset of
A
— and codomain
\{0,1\}
, such that

•

f
fulfils the inductive properties of Tarski’s definition of the satisfaction relation, applied to the structure
(A;\in,\vec{R},\Sigma)
,

•

y=\{\phi\in\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}:f(\phi)=1\}
,

•

\Gamma\subset y
,

•

p\subset\Sigma
, and

•

``\Sigma\text{ is }\mathcal{L}\text{-nice}"
,

every of which aforementioned points is expressible as
\Delta_{0}
formulas (following Definition
2.7
) in the language of set theory. Note also that whenever
\varphi(\Sigma,y)
holds,
\Sigma
,
y
and any witness
f
must have transitive closures of cardinalities no larger than
\lambda
. Moreover, we have

\psi\iff\exists\Sigma\ \exists y\ \varphi(\Sigma,y).

As
p,\mathfrak{A},\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}},\Gamma
are subsets of
trcl((p,\mathfrak{A},\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}},\Gamma))
and
|trcl((p,\mathfrak{A},\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}},\Gamma))|\leq\lambda
, the structure

\mathfrak{B}:=(trcl((p,\mathfrak{A},\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}},\Gamma));\in,p,\mathfrak{A},\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}},\Gamma)

can be coded as a real in
V[g]
, by Lemma
2.15
. This means that in all weak outer models of
V[g]
,
\psi
can be thought of as a
\mathbf{\Sigma^{1}_{1}}
sentence involving a real code of
\mathfrak{B}
found in
V[g]
. In particular, by Mostowski’s absoluteness theorem,
\psi
is absolute for
V[g]
and
W[g]
. Now
W[g]\models\psi
implies
V[g]\models\psi
, contradicting (
3.6
).
∎

Note that the proof of Lemma
3.55
does not require that
\Gamma
contains only
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentences. Indeed, for a litany of properties
K
, the existence of an object satisfying
K
is absolute between
V
and its weak outer models and hence, between
V
and its forcing extensions. However, it is often useful — if not integral — to have a proper handle on such an object. It is towards this end that we are often interested in the existence of a
V
-generic object
k
such that
k
satisfies
K
in
V[k]
.

Specifying “
k
satisfies
K
” to be “
k\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\Gamma
”, the following lemma is thus well-motivated.

Lemma 3.56
.

Let
W
,
\lambda
,
B
,
P
,
\mathbb{P}
and
g
be such that

•

|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|\leq\lambda
,

•

B
is
\mathcal{L}
-closed under finite extensions,

•

P=\{p\in B:\ \Vdash_{Col(\omega,\lambda)}\exists\Sigma\ (``\Sigma\ \Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }p")\}
,

•

\mathbb{P}=(P,\supset\cap\ P)
,

•

\mathbb{P}
is
\Sigma_{1}
-definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
,

•

W
is a weak outer model of
V
, and

•

g\in W
is a
\mathbb{P}
-
\Sigma_{1}
-generic filter over
\mathfrak{A}
.

If there is
\Sigma
in a weak outer model
W^{\prime}
of
V
such that
\Sigma

\Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
\emptyset
, then
\bigcup g

\Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
\emptyset
.

In particular, if
g
is
\mathbb{P}
-generic over
V
, then
\bigcup g

\Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
\emptyset
in
V[g]=V[\bigcup g]
.

Proof.

The general statement is clear from Lemma
3.42
, Remark
3.47
, Proposition
3.54
and Lemma
3.55
. That
\bigcup g

\Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
\emptyset
is absolute for transitive models of
\mathsf{ZFC-Powerset}
, so if
g
is
\mathbb{P}
-generic over
V
, then
\bigcup g

\Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
\emptyset
in
V[g]
. Moreover, since
g=[\bigcup g]^{<\omega}
, we have
V[g]=V[\bigcup g]
.
∎

We are now equipped to tackle
(Q0)
. In the presence of Proposition
3.54
, the next lemma implies an affirmative answer to
(Q0)
.

Lemma 3.57
.

There are
W
,
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
P^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
,
g
and
\phi
such that

(1)

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is closed under negation,

(2)

\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
-suitable,

(3)

\Gamma^{\prime}
is a set of
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentences,

(4)

P^{\prime}=\{p\in[\mathcal{L}^{\prime}]^{<\omega}:\ \Vdash_{Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A}^{\prime})|)}\exists\Sigma\ (``\Sigma\ \Gamma^{\prime}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathfrak{A}^{\prime})\text{-certifies }p")\}\neq\emptyset
,

(5)

\mathbb{P}^{\prime}=(P^{\prime},\supset\cap\ P^{\prime})
,

(6)

\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
is
\Sigma_{1}
-definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
,

(7)

W
is a weak outer model of
V
,

(8)

g\in W
is a
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-generic filter over
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
,

(9)

\phi
is a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
-
\Sigma_{2}
sentence which is
(\mathfrak{A}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
-universal, and

(10)

\bigcup g\not\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}\phi
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
be the closure under negation of the following set of expressions (strings):

\{\ulcorner\dot{F}(\alpha)=\beta\urcorner:(\alpha,\beta)\in\omega_{1}\times\omega_{2}\}

Clearly,
(1)
holds. Set
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
to be
(H((2^{\omega_{2}})^{+});\in)
, so that
(2)
holds. Using a natural notational shorthand for passing parameters into
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
formulas (see Remark
4.14
for more information), define

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}:=\{

\displaystyle\ulcorner\forall\alpha<\omega_{1}\ \exists\beta\ (E(\ulcorner\dot{F}(\alpha)=\beta\urcorner))\urcorner,

\displaystyle\ulcorner\forall\alpha\ \forall\beta\ \forall\gamma\ ((E(\ulcorner\dot{F}(\alpha)=\beta\urcorner)\wedge E(\ulcorner\dot{F}(\alpha)=\gamma\urcorner))\implies\beta=\gamma)\urcorner\}\text{,}

and observe that
(3)
is satisfied (refer again to Remark
4.14
for justification of the shorthand not underselling the complexity of any
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
-
\Pi_{2}
formula it abbreviates).

Next, set

\displaystyle P^{\prime}:=\{p\in[\mathcal{L}^{\prime}]^{<\omega}:\ \Vdash_{Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A}^{\prime})|)}\exists\Sigma\ (``\Sigma\ \Gamma^{\prime}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathfrak{A}^{\prime})\text{-certifies }p")\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\prime}:=(P^{\prime},\supset\cap\ P^{\prime})

to satisfy
(5)
and
(6)
, since
[\mathcal{L}^{\prime}]^{<\omega}\in H((2^{\omega_{2}})^{+})
implies
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}\in H((2^{\omega_{2}})^{+})
.

Definition 3.58
.

Given a set
X
, let
\Sigma(X)
denote

\displaystyle\{\ulcorner\dot{F}(\alpha)=\beta\urcorner:(\alpha,\beta)\in(\omega_{1}\times\omega_{2})\cap X\}\ \cup

\displaystyle\{\ulcorner\neg\dot{F}(\alpha)=\beta\urcorner:(\alpha,\beta)\in(\omega_{1}\times\omega_{2})\setminus X\}\text{.}

Note that for any
Z
in an weak outer model of
V
,

Z\cap(\omega_{1}\times\omega_{2})\text{ is a function from }\omega_{1}\text{ into }\omega_{2}\iff\Sigma(Z)\ \Gamma^{\prime}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathfrak{A}^{\prime})\text{-certifies }\emptyset\text{.}

As there already exist functions from
\omega_{1}
into
\omega_{2}
in
V
,
\emptyset\in P^{\prime}
by Lemma
3.55
, giving us
(4)
. It suffices to show that
(7)
to
(10)
hold for

•

any
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
-generic filter
g
over
V
(and thus over
\mathfrak{A}
),

•

W:=V[g]
, and

•

\phi:=\ulcorner\exists f\in{{}^{\omega}{\omega_{2}}}\ \forall n<\omega\ (E(\ulcorner\dot{F}(n)=f(n)\urcorner))\urcorner
.

Trivially,
(7)
and
(8)
are done. Further,
\phi
is a
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}
-
\Sigma_{2}
sentence, seeing that
{{}^{\omega}{\omega_{2}}}\in H((2^{\omega_{2}})^{+})
.

Choose any
p\in P^{\prime}
. It is not hard to verify that there exists

p^{*}\in Col(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=(\bigcup\{{{}^{\alpha}{\omega_{2}}}:\alpha<\omega_{1}\},\supset)

for which
p\subset\Sigma(q^{*})
whenever
q^{*}
extends
p^{*}
in
Col(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
. Let
h
be a
Col(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})
-generic filter over
V
containing
p^{*}
. Then in
V[h]
,

\Sigma(\bigcup h)\ (\Gamma^{\prime}\cup\{\phi\})(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathfrak{A}^{\prime})\text{-certifies }p\text{.}

According to Proposition
3.54
, we arrive at
(9)
.

Finally, choose any
f^{*}\in{{}^{\omega}{\omega_{2}}}
. Due to the straightforward observation that

\displaystyle D_{f^{*}}:=\{p\in P^{\prime}:\exists n<\omega\ \exists\beta<\omega_{2}\ (

\displaystyle(\ulcorner\dot{F}(n)=\beta\urcorner\in p\wedge f(n)\neq\beta)\ \vee

\displaystyle(\ulcorner\neg\dot{F}(n)=\beta\urcorner\in p\wedge f(n)=\beta))\}

is dense in
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
, we must have
\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}\varphi_{f^{*}}
, where

\varphi_{f^{*}}:=\ulcorner\neg\forall n<\omega\ (E(\ulcorner\dot{F}(n)=f^{*}(n)\urcorner))\urcorner\text{.}

Having
f^{*}
range over
{{}^{\omega}{\omega_{2}}}
then nets us
(10)
.
∎

This subsection shall be concluded with another absoluteness result. This time, instead of looking for witnesses in forcing extensions, we turn our focus to the forcing notions themselves.

Lemma 3.59
.

The definition of
\mathbb{P}
from parameters
B
,
\mathcal{L}
,
\mathfrak{A}
,
\Gamma
in Proposition
3.54
, where
\lambda
is additionally specified to be
|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|
, is absolute for transitive models of
\mathsf{ZFC}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the set

\{p\in B:\ \Vdash_{Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|)}\exists\Sigma\ (``\Sigma\ \Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }p")\}

(3.7)

is absolute for transitive models of
\mathsf{ZFC}
.

Let
V^{\prime}
and
W
be transitive models of
\mathsf{ZFC}
such that
\{B,\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A},\Gamma\}\subset V^{\prime}\subset W
. Have
P^{V^{\prime}}
and
P^{W}
denote the versions of the set (
3.7
) defined in
V^{\prime}
and
W
respectively. We want to prove
P^{V^{\prime}}=P^{W}
.

First note that

Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|)^{V^{\prime}}=Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|^{V^{\prime}})\cong Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|^{W})=Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|)^{W}

in
W
, so

p\in P^{W}\iff\ \Vdash_{Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|)^{V^{\prime}}}\exists\Sigma\ (``\Sigma\ \Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }p")

(3.8)

in
W
. Since any forcing extension of
W
is a weak outer model of
V
, a direct application of Lemma
3.55
gives us
P^{W}\subset P^{V^{\prime}}
. Next, fix any
p\in P^{V^{\prime}}
and any
Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|)^{V^{\prime}}
-generic filter
g
over
W
. Now
g
is also a
Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|)^{V^{\prime}}
-generic filter over
V^{\prime}
, so
V^{\prime}[g]
and
W[g]
are transitive models of
\mathsf{ZFC}
and moreover,
V^{\prime}[g]\subset W[g]
. By the definition of
P^{V^{\prime}}
in
V^{\prime}
,

V^{\prime}[g]\models``\Sigma\ \Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }p"

for some
\Sigma\in V^{\prime}[g]
. That
\Sigma\ \Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})
-certifying
p
is absolute for transitive models of
\mathsf{ZFC}
implies

W[g]\models``\Sigma\ \Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }p".

We have thus shown

W\models(\Vdash_{Col(\omega,|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|)^{V^{\prime}}}\exists\Sigma\ (``\Sigma\ \Gamma(\mathcal{L},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }p")),

whence
p\in P^{W}
by (
3.8
). This allows us to conclude
P^{V^{\prime}}=P^{W}
.
∎

4
Extending Namba Forcing

This section illustrates how the framework introduced in Subsection
3.3
can be applied to resolve some problems in set theory.

4.1
An Extension Problem

Before stating our problem of interest, we feel obliged to present, at least in brief, the history surrounding it.

Fix a limit ordinal
\alpha
and consider the chain of inequalities

cof(\alpha)\leq|\alpha|\leq\alpha\text{,}

(4.1)

which is provable in
\mathsf{ZFC}
. Set theorists have long investigated the ability to change the signs in (
4.1
) via forcing. If
|\alpha|<\alpha
in
V
, then the same must hold in any forcing extension . If
cof(\alpha)<|\alpha|
in
V
, we can always force
cof(\alpha)=|\alpha|
by collapsing both
cof(\alpha)
and
|\alpha|
to a regular cardinal in
V
no greater than
cof(\alpha)
.

On the flipside, if
|\alpha|=\alpha
in
V
, then
\alpha
is a cardinal there. As long as
\alpha
is uncountable, a forcing notion that collapses
\alpha
(to
\omega
, say) exists and necessarily forces
|\alpha|<\alpha
. We are left with the case where
cof(\alpha)=|\alpha|
in
V
. Note that by swapping
\alpha
with a smaller ordinal if necessary, we can assume
\alpha
is regular in
V
without loss of generality. So assume
\alpha
is an uncountable regular cardinal in
V
. If there is a singular cardinal
\beta
below
\alpha
, one can simply collapse
\alpha
to
\beta
to achieve
cof(\alpha)<|\alpha|
, since the usual forcing notion for this purpose preserves the cardinality of
\beta
. Otherwise, forcing
cof(\alpha)<|\alpha|
appears to be highly non-trivial.

In his doctoral dissertation
[
3
]
, Prikry assumed
\alpha
is a measurable cardinal, and gave an example of a forcing notion that preserves all cardinalities, yet changes
cof(\alpha)
to
\omega
. A natural follow-up question to Prikry’s result is thus:

can we force the the separation of
cof(\alpha)
and
|\alpha|
on an uncountable regular
\alpha
which provably exists over
\mathsf{ZFC}
?

As successor cardinals are the only uncountable regular cardinals proven to exist over
\mathsf{ZFC}
, a forcing notion separating
cof(\alpha)
and
|\alpha|
for any such
\alpha
must collapse
\alpha
. But can we ensure
\alpha
is not collapsed “too far”? In other words, we want to force
cof(\alpha)<|\alpha|
while preserving all cardinals below
\alpha
.

The late 1960s saw two independent solutions to this problem in the affirmative, by Bukovský
[
6
]
and Namba
[
4
]
. Both solutions work with
\alpha=\omega_{2}
, which is the smallest possible value
\alpha
can take in an affirmative answer. Simplifications were made to the presentation of Bukovský’s and Namba’s forcing notions over the years, without losing sight of the goal of their constructions. These simplifications culminated in what is now commonly known as
Namba forcing
. Since the focus of this section is on extending the key effects of Namba forcing, we feel obliged to define the forcing notion for the sake of completeness.

Definition 4.1
.

We say
(T,\leq)
is a
\kappa
-splitting in

A
iff

•

(T,\leq)
is a tree, and

•

for every
s\in T\cap A
,
s
has
\kappa
many immediate
\leq
-successors in
T
.

Definition 4.2
.

If
(T,\leq)
is a partial order and
s\in T
, we use
T_{s}^{\leq}
to denote the set of
\leq
-successors of
s
in
T
. More formally,

T_{s}^{\leq}:=\{t\in T:s\leq t\}.

Definition 4.3
.

Define the order
\leq^{\dagger}
to be

\{(s,t)\in\omega_{2}^{<\omega}\times\omega_{2}^{<\omega}:dom(s)\subset dom(t)\text{ and }t\!\restriction_{dom(s)}=s\}.

Definition 4.4
.

A
Namba tree
is a subset
T
of
\omega_{2}^{<\omega}
containing a
root

s
such that

•

(T,\leq^{\dagger})
is
\omega_{2}
-splitting in
T_{s}^{\leq^{\dagger}}
, and

•

whenever
t\in T
, either
s\leq^{\dagger}t
or
t\leq^{\dagger}s
.

Definition 4.5

(Namba)
.

Define

\displaystyle P_{N}:=\

\displaystyle\{T\subset\omega_{2}^{<\omega}:T\text{ is a Namba tree}\}\text{, and}

\displaystyle\leq_{N}\ :=\

\displaystyle\{(p,q)\in P_{N}\times P_{N}:p\subset q\}.

We call the forcing notion
\mathbb{P}_{N}:=(P_{N},\leq_{N})

Namba forcing
.

Namba forcing belongs to the class of
uniformly-splitting tree forcings
, one of which earliest-known members is Mathias forcing. A typical condition of a uniformly-splitting tree forcing is a tree, and it can be divided into two components, the stem and the crown. The stem is the main working part of a condition; stems in a generic filter combine to form a function that is the primary generic object we desire. The crowns work as
side conditions
, which in unity, endow the forcing notion with specific regularity properties. These properties are often crucial to the satisfaction of constraints placed on the forcing extension. If
T
is a Namba tree with root
s
, then its stem is

\{t\in T:t\subset s\}

and its crown is
T_{s}^{\leq^{\dagger}}\setminus\{s\}
.

By means of tree combinatorics, one can show that Namba forcing gives
\omega_{2}^{V}
a cofinality of
\omega
without collapsing
\omega_{1}^{V}
. In fact, Namba forcing is a textbook example of such a forcing notion. It also has a stronger property than not collapsing
\omega_{1}^{V}
, for it is stationary-preserving. In the parlance of the preceding paragraph, the primary generic object here is a cofinal function from
\omega
into
\omega_{2}^{V}
, whereas the regularity property of pertinence is being stationary-preserving. We can then observe the following division of labour: the stems of Namba forcing are in charge of changing the cofinality of
\omega_{2}^{V}
to
\omega
, while the crowns of Namba forcing ensure all stationary subsets of
\omega_{1}
in
V
have their stationarity preserved.

The extended Namba problem, at its most rudimentary, asks (in
V
) for which ordinals
\lambda>\omega_{2}
is the statement

\displaystyle Nb_{0}(\lambda):=\text{`}

\displaystyle\text{there is a stationary-preserving forcing notion }\mathbb{P}\text{ such that}

\displaystyle\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}``cof(\alpha)=\omega"\text{ for all regular cardinals }\alpha\text{ satisfying }\omega_{2}\leq\alpha<\lambda\text{'}

true. It is easy to see that if
\lambda>\omega_{2}
is not a regular cardinal, then

Nb_{0}(\lambda)\iff Nb_{0}(\lambda^{+})\text{,}

so it suffices to only consider
Nb_{0}(\lambda)
for regular cardinals
\lambda>\omega_{2}
.

Fact 4.6
.

Namba forcing witnesses
Nb_{0}(\omega_{3})
.

This formulation of the problem is already non-trivial, because finding witnesses to
Nb_{0}(\lambda)
for
\lambda>\omega_{3}
turns out to be nearly as difficult as proving
\neg Nb_{0}(\lambda)
. Perhaps as a sign of this difficulty, the following fact tells us that iterating Namba forcing in the usual way is insufficient to get us
Nb_{0}(\lambda)
for any
\lambda>\omega_{3}
, without assuming a strong failure of
\mathsf{GCH}
.

Fact 4.7
.

Assume
\mathsf{GCH}
holds below
\omega_{2}
. Then

(1)

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{N}}``cof(\omega_{3}^{V})=\omega_{1}"
, and

(2)

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{N}}``cof(\beta)>\omega"
for all regular cardinals
\beta
satisfying
\omega_{3}<\beta
.

Notice that if
\mathsf{GCH}
holds below
\omega_{2}
, then by Fact
4.7
, iterating Namba forcing any number of times in the standard sense would not result in
cof(\omega_{3}^{V})=\omega
, not without collapsing
\omega_{1}^{V}
.

Remark 4.8
.

That any standard iteration of Namba forcing fails to extend
Nb_{0}(\lambda)
beyond
\lambda=\omega_{3}
, assuming the hypothesis of Fact
4.7
, suggests our natural conception of iteration is incompatible with the side conditions of Namba forcing. Indeed,
(1)
of Fact
4.7
is a result of interactions between the hypothesis of said fact, and the behaviour of these side conditions.

To overcome this incompatibility, it makes sense to consider either

(a)

a new kind of iteration, or

(b)

an overhaul of the side conditions.

Option
(a)
is almost unfathomable, since the typical intuitions to — and (informal) definitions of — iterated forcing necessitates that an iterated forcing notion be a regular extension of each of its initial iterands. In other words, an iterated Namba forcing extension ought to include a Namba forcing extension as a submodel. This forbids an iteration of Namba forcing from witnessing
Nb_{0}(\lambda)
for any
\lambda>\omega_{3}
, should
\mathsf{GCH}
hold below
\omega_{2}
.

On the other hand, option
(b)
could mean a departure from the intuition of uniformly-splitting tree forcings so radical, that the resultant forcing notion has conditions best presented as objects other than trees.

Indeed, a stronger variant of the extended Namba problem, asking for which regular cardinals
\lambda>\omega_{2}
is the statement

\displaystyle Nb_{1}(\lambda):=\text{`}

\displaystyle\text{there is a stationary-preserving forcing notion }\mathbb{P}\text{ such that}

\displaystyle\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}``cof(\alpha)=\omega"\text{ for all regular cardinals }\alpha\text{ satisfying }\omega_{2}\leq\alpha<\lambda\text{,}

\displaystyle\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}``cof(\lambda)=\omega_{1}"\text{, and}

\displaystyle\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}``cof(\beta)>\omega"\text{ for all regular cardinals }\beta\text{ satisfying }\lambda<\beta\text{'}

true, naturally arises from Fact
4.7
. Indeed, Fact
4.7
is equivalent to — and can be rewritten as — the following.

Fact 4.7
′
.

Assume
\mathsf{GCH}
holds below
\omega_{2}
. Then Namba forcing witnesses
Nb_{1}(\omega_{3})
.

Drawing from the deep and complex theories of subcomplete forcing and
\mathcal{L}
-forcing, Jensen showed in
[
15
]
that, modulo weak fragments of
\mathsf{GCH}
,
Nb_{1}(\lambda)
holds for all successor and strongly inaccessible cardinals above
\omega_{2}
. Jensen used very different methods to construct the witnesses
\mathbb{P}
for different categories of
\lambda
, but in doing so, he also ensured that
\mathbb{P}
never adds reals.

But can we have
Nb_{1}(\lambda)
hold for a bigger class of cardinals
\lambda
if we allow
\mathbb{P}
to add reals?

4.2
A Conditional Solution

It turns out there is a somewhat simple proof of

\text{``}Nb_{1}(\lambda)\text{ for every regular cardinal }\lambda>\omega_{2}\text{''}

(in fact, a slightly stronger statement) if we assume a theory of greater consistency strength than
\mathsf{ZFC}
. This proof adopts a novel side-condition technique first employed in
[
16
]
(cf. Remark
4.8
). It also demonstrates how amenable the forcing framework of Subsection
3.3
is in bolstering natural and obvious forcing conditions with said side conditions.

Definition 4.9
.

Let
Nb^{\prime}_{1}(\lambda)
denote the statement

‘

\displaystyle\text{there is a stationary-preserving forcing notion }\mathbb{P}\text{ such that}

\displaystyle\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}``cof(\alpha)=\omega"\text{ for all regular cardinals }\alpha\text{ satisfying }\omega_{2}\leq\alpha<\lambda\text{,}

\displaystyle\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}``cof(\gamma)=\omega_{1}"\text{ for all regular cardinals }\gamma\text{ satisfying }\lambda\leq\gamma\leq 2^{<\lambda}\text{, and}

\displaystyle\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}``cof(\beta)=\beta"\text{ for all regular cardinals }\beta\text{ satisfying }2^{<\lambda}<\beta\text{'.}

Immediately, one can see that
Nb^{\prime}_{1}(\lambda)
implies
Nb_{1}(\lambda)
whenever
\lambda>\omega_{2}
is a regular cardinal. By generalising the (redacted) proof of Fact
4.7
(or equivalently, Fact
4.7′
), we can show that statements in the class

\{Nb^{\prime}_{1}(\lambda):\lambda>\omega_{2}\}

are indeed extensions of a property of Namba forcing, modulo the same mild assumption beyond
\mathsf{ZFC}
.

Fact 4.10
.

Assume
\mathsf{GCH}
holds below
\omega_{2}
. Then Namba forcing witnesses
Nb^{\prime}_{1}(\omega_{3})
.

The next theorem, also the main one in this section, tells us
Nb^{\prime}_{1}(\lambda)
holds for all but set many regular cardinals
\lambda
, under significantly stronger assumptions.

Theorem 4.11
.

Assume
\mathrm{NS}_{\omega_{1}}
is precipitous. Then
Nb^{\prime}_{1}(\lambda_{f})
holds for all regular cardinals
\lambda_{f}>2^{\omega_{1}}
. In other words, whenever
\lambda_{f}>2^{\omega_{1}}
is a regular cardinal, there is a stationary-preserving forcing notion
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
such that

(1)

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}``cof(\alpha)=\omega"
for all regular cardinals
\alpha
satisfying
\omega_{2}\leq\alpha<\lambda_{f}
,

(2)

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}``cof(\gamma)=\omega_{1}"
for all regular cardinals
\gamma
satisfying
\lambda_{f}\leq\gamma\leq 2^{<\lambda_{f}}
, and

(3)

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}``cof(\beta)=\beta"
for all regular cardinals
\beta
satisfying
2^{<\lambda_{f}}<\beta
.

Proof.

Assume
\mathrm{NS}_{\omega_{1}}
is precipitous, and fix a regular cardinal
\lambda_{f}>2^{\omega_{1}}
.

Let
h
be a generic filter on
Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})
, so that in
V[h]
,

•

cof(\alpha)=cof^{V}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha\leq\lambda_{f}
,

•

\mathrm{NS}_{\omega_{1}}
is still precipitous,

•

|H(\lambda_{f})|=\lambda_{f}
, and

•

there is a a
\Diamond_{\lambda_{f}}
-sequence
(\bar{A}_{\lambda}:\lambda<\lambda_{f})
.

Lemma 4.12
.

If in
V[h]
there is a stationary-preserving forcing notion
\mathbb{P}
of size
\leq\lambda_{f}
fulfilling

(1’)

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}``cof(\alpha)=\omega"
for all regular cardinals
\alpha
satisfying
\omega_{2}\leq\alpha<\lambda_{f}
, and

(2’)

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}``cof(\lambda_{f})=\omega_{1}"
,

then for some
Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})
-name
\dot{\mathbb{P}}
for
\mathbb{P}
,

Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})*\dot{\mathbb{P}}\text{,}

is a stationary-preserving forcing notion fulfilling
(1)
to
(3)
of Theorem
4.11
in
V
.

Proof.

Working in
V
, we set

\mathbb{P}^{\prime}:=Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})*\dot{\mathbb{P}}\text{,}

and note the following facts:

(3’)

|Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})|=2^{<\lambda_{f}}
,

(4’)

\Vdash_{Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})}``2^{<\lambda_{f}}=\lambda_{f}"

\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
is a stationary-preserving forcing notion fulfilling
(1)
of Theorem
4.11
because

•

Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})
is a stationary-preserving forcing notion forcing
\dot{\mathbb{P}}
to be stationary-preserving,

•

Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})
forces
cof(\alpha)=cof^{V}(\alpha)
for all
\alpha<\lambda_{f}
, and

•

(1’)
holds.

In
V^{Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})}
, we are given
|\mathbb{P}|\leq\lambda_{f}
, so
\mathbb{P}
preserves cofinalities
\geq\lambda_{f}^{+}
. But
\lambda_{f}^{+}
in
V^{Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})}
is exactly
(2^{<\lambda_{f}})^{+}
in
V
, by
(3’)
and
(4’)
, so
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
preserves cofinalities
\geq(2^{<\lambda_{f}})^{+}
in
V
. This implies
(3)
of Theorem
4.11
.

Now let
\gamma\geq\lambda_{f}
be regular in
V
. Then
cof^{Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})}(\gamma)\geq\lambda_{f}
because
Col(\lambda_{f},\lambda_{f})
is
\lambda_{f}
-closed. That
\mathbb{P}
both preserves cofinalities
\geq\lambda_{f}^{+}
and fulfils
(2’)
implies it forces
cof(\gamma)\geq\omega_{1}
. We have thus shown

(5’)

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}``cof(\gamma)\geq\omega_{1}"
for all regular cardinals
\gamma
satisfying
\lambda_{f}\leq\gamma
.

As
(2’)
,
(4’)
and
(1)
of Theorem
4.11
give us

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}``cof(\gamma)\leq|\gamma|\leq\omega_{1}"

for all ordinals
\gamma
satisfying
\lambda_{f}\leq\gamma\leq 2^{<\lambda_{f}}
,
(2)
of Theorem
4.11
must hold.
∎

Allow
W
to denote
V[h]
. Unless otherwise stated, we work in
W
from now on, towards a forcing notion
\mathbb{P}
as in Lemma
4.12
. For brevity, we write
\omega_{1}^{V}
as just
\omega_{1}
. Let

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle:=(2^{\lambda_{f}})^{+}\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}

\displaystyle:=(H(\kappa);\in)\text{, and}

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle:=\{i<\lambda_{f}:\omega_{2}\leq i\text{ and }i\text{ is regular}\}.

As
|H(\lambda_{f})|=\lambda_{f}
, we can fix a bijection
c:\lambda_{f}\longrightarrow H(\lambda_{f})
, and define

•

Q_{\lambda}:=c"\lambda
and

•

A_{\lambda}:=c"(\bar{A}_{\lambda})

for each
\lambda<\lambda_{f}
.

Making use of straightforward closure arguments, we inductively define
C
such that

•

C
is a club in
\lambda_{f}
, and

•

for all
\lambda\in C
,

\circ

Q_{\lambda}
is transitive,

\circ

(Q_{\lambda};\in,c\cap Q_{\lambda})\prec(H(\lambda_{f});\in,c)
.

We will let
Q_{\lambda_{f}}=H(\lambda_{f})
.

Now, given any
P,B\subset H(\lambda_{f})
, the set

\displaystyle\{\lambda\in C:(Q_{\lambda};\in,P,B)\prec(H(\lambda_{f});\in,P,B)\}

is a club in
\lambda_{f}
. Moreover, we can derive from
(\bar{A}_{\lambda}:\lambda<\lambda_{f})
being a
\Diamond_{\lambda_{f}}
-sequence, that the set

\displaystyle\{\lambda\in C:B\cap Q_{\lambda}=A_{\lambda}\}

is stationary in
\lambda_{f}
. We thus obtain

(
\diamond
)

for all
P,B\subset H(\lambda_{f})
, the set

\displaystyle\{\lambda\in C:(Q_{\lambda};\in,P,A_{\lambda})\prec(H(\lambda_{f});\in,P,B)\}

is stationary in
\lambda_{f}
.

We want to define
\mathbb{P}
as a forcing notion comprising finite fragments of some language
\mathcal{L}\subset H(\lambda_{f})
, such that
\mathbb{P}
satisfies the hypothesis of Lemma
4.12
.

Let us first define
\mathcal{L}
.

Definition 4.13
.

The language
\mathcal{L}
requires the following distinguished symbols:

•

\dot{F}_{i}
for
i\in R
, and

•

\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda}
for
\delta<\omega_{1}
and
\lambda\in C
.

Now fix
\mathcal{L}
to be the smallest set closed under negation, that contains expressions (strings) of the following types:

(L1)

\ulcorner\dot{F}_{i}(n)=\alpha\urcorner
, for
i\in R
,
n<\omega
and
\alpha<i
, and

(L2)

\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda}\urcorner
, for
\delta<\omega_{1}
,
\lambda\in C
and
x\in Q_{\lambda}
.

Morally, each
\dot{F}_{i}
labels an increasing and cofinal partial map from
f(i)
into
i
, and each
\dot{X}_{\delta}
labels a side condition. The side conditions will be used to preserve stationary subsets of
\omega_{1}
. As

•

\mathfrak{A}
is a transitive model of
\mathsf{ZFC-Powerset}
, and

•

\mathcal{L}\subset H(\lambda_{f})\in H(\kappa)
,

\mathfrak{A}
is
\mathcal{L}
-suitable.

One may argue that the remark below has been a long time coming, considering it was referenced in the proof of Lemma
3.57
.

Remark 4.14
.

Sometimes, we want to pass certain parameters of an expression in
\mathcal{L}
via variables. In such circumstances we are formally passing the parameters through the functions

\chi_{1}:

(i,n,\alpha)\mapsto\ulcorner\dot{F}_{i}(n)=\alpha\urcorner
,

\chi_{2}:

(\delta,\lambda,x)\mapsto\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda}\urcorner
,

\chi_{3}:

(i,n,\alpha)\mapsto\ulcorner\neg\dot{F}_{i}(n)=\alpha\urcorner
, and

\chi_{4}:

(\delta,\lambda,x)\mapsto\ulcorner\neg x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda}\urcorner
,

with their domains restricted to
H(\lambda_{f})
. Note that under this domain restriction,
\chi_{1}
,
\chi_{2}
,
\chi_{3}
and
\chi_{4}
are all members of
H(\kappa)
; in fact, they are all
\Delta_{0}
-definable functions in
(H(\lambda_{f});\in)
(and thus in
\mathfrak{A}
). For brevity’s and clarity’s sake, we will abuse notation and suppress mention of these functions, whenever it is clear that we are using variables as placeholders for parameters in our construction of
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
formulas involving the symbol
\ulcorner E\urcorner
— see e.g. Definition
4.20
. Since

•

for
k\in\{1,2,3,4\}
and
(a,b,c)\in dom(\chi_{k})
,

\text{``}E(\chi_{k}(a,b,c))\text{''}

can be viewed as a shorthand for both

\ulcorner\exists z\ (\varphi\wedge E(z))\urcorner

and

\ulcorner\forall z\ (\varphi\implies E(z))\urcorner,

where
\varphi
is a
\Delta_{0}
formula (going by Definition
2.7
) in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
expressing the statement

\text{``}\chi_{k}(a,b,c)=z\text{'',}

and

•

\Delta_{0}
formulas in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
is of the lowest complexity class (
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Delta_{0}
) in our classification of
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
formulas,

by an argument similar to the main one of Remark
3.34
, this method of passing parameters incurs no additional cost to the complexity of any
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Sigma_{1}
(and hence also
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
) formula thus abbreviated.

By our choice of
C
, if
\lambda\in C
, then
\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda}
is precisely the smallest set closed under negation, that contains expressions of the following types:

(L1)
λ

\ulcorner\dot{F}_{i}(n)=\alpha\urcorner
, for
i\in R\cap\lambda
,
n<\omega
and
\alpha<i
, and

(L2)
λ

\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner
, for
\delta<\omega_{1}
,
\lambda^{\prime}\in C\cap\lambda
and
x\in Q_{\lambda^{\prime}}
.

Definition 4.15
.

For any
\lambda\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}
, an object of the form

\langle\langle F_{i}:i\in Z\rangle,\langle X_{\delta,\lambda}:\delta<\omega_{1},\lambda\in C\rangle\rangle

interprets

\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda}
iff

•

Z=R\cap Q_{\lambda}
,

•

each
F_{i}
is a partial function from
\omega
into
i
, and

•

each
X_{\delta,\lambda}
is a subset of
Q_{\lambda}
.

Sometimes it is convenient to talk about interpretations of specific symbols occurring in
\mathcal{L}
.

Definition 4.16
.

For any pair
(i,\Sigma)
, define
F_{i}(\Sigma)
to be the set

\{(n,\alpha):\ulcorner\dot{F}_{i}(n)=\alpha\urcorner\in\Sigma\}.

Definition 4.17
.

For any triple
(\delta,\lambda,\Sigma)
, define
X_{\delta,\lambda}(\Sigma)
to be the set

\{x:\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda}\urcorner\in\Sigma\}.

Definition 4.18
.

Given
\lambda\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}
,

\mathfrak{C}:=\langle\langle F_{i}:i\in Z\rangle,\langle X_{\delta,\lambda}:\delta<\omega_{1},\lambda\in C\rangle\rangle

interpreting
\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{L}
, let
\Sigma(\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
denote the union of the following sets:

•

\{\ulcorner\dot{F}_{i}(n)=\alpha\urcorner\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}:F_{i}(n)=\alpha\}
,

•

\{\ulcorner\neg\dot{F}_{i}(n)=\alpha\urcorner\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}:F_{i}(n)\neq\alpha\}
,

•

\{\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda}\urcorner\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}:x\in X_{\delta,\lambda}\}
, and

•

\{\ulcorner\neg x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda}\urcorner\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}:x\not\in X_{\delta,\lambda}\}
.

It is clear that
\Sigma(\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{L})\cap Q_{\lambda}=\Sigma(\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda})
is
\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda}
-nice for all
\lambda\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}
.

Remark 4.19
.

If

•

\lambda\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}
,

•

\Sigma
is
\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda}
-nice, and

•

\mathfrak{C}:=\langle\langle F_{i}(\Sigma):i\in R\cap\lambda\rangle,\langle X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}(\Sigma):\delta<\omega_{1},\lambda^{\prime}\in C\cap\lambda\rangle\rangle
interprets
\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda}
,

then
\Sigma(\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda})=\Sigma
.

We will define
\{\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}:\lambda\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}\}
by induction on
\lambda
. Assume that
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
has been defined for all
\lambda^{\prime}\in\lambda\cap C
. Also, for
\lambda^{\prime}\in\lambda\cap C
, allow

•

\mathcal{L^{*}}
to denote the set of first order formulas over the signature
\{\in,P,B\}
,

•

Ef_{0}^{\lambda}(\lambda^{\prime})
to denote the set

\displaystyle\{(\phi,\bar{s},r):\

\displaystyle\phi\in\mathcal{L}^{*}\text{ and}

\displaystyle r\in Q_{\lambda^{\prime}}\text{ and}

\displaystyle\bar{s}\in(Q_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{<\omega}\text{ and}

\displaystyle dom(\bar{s})+1=arity(\phi)\text{ and}

\displaystyle(Q_{\lambda^{\prime}};\in,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}},A_{\lambda^{\prime}})\models\phi(r,\bar{s})\},

•

Ef_{1}^{\lambda}(\lambda^{\prime})
to denote the set

\displaystyle\{(\phi,\bar{s}):\exists r\ ((\phi,\bar{s},r)\in Ef_{0}(\lambda^{\prime}))\},

and

•

Df^{\lambda}(\lambda^{\prime})
to denote the set

\displaystyle\{(\phi,\bar{s}):\

\displaystyle\phi\in\mathcal{L}^{*}\text{ and}

\displaystyle\bar{s}\in(Q_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{<\omega}\text{ and}

\displaystyle dom(\bar{s})+1=arity(\phi)\text{ and}

\displaystyle\{y\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}:(Q_{\lambda^{\prime}};\in,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}},A_{\lambda^{\prime}})\models\phi(y,\bar{s})\}\text{ is dense in }\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\}.

The functions

\displaystyle Ef_{0}^{\lambda}:\

\displaystyle\lambda\cap C\longrightarrow H(\lambda_{f})\ [\lambda^{\prime}\mapsto Ef_{0}(\lambda^{\prime})]\text{,}

\displaystyle Ef_{1}^{\lambda}:\

\displaystyle\lambda\cap C\longrightarrow H(\lambda_{f})\ [\lambda^{\prime}\mapsto Ef_{1}(\lambda^{\prime})]\text{, and}

\displaystyle Df^{\lambda}:\

\displaystyle\lambda\cap C\longrightarrow H(\lambda_{f})\ [\lambda^{\prime}\mapsto Df(\lambda^{\prime})]

are clearly members of
H(\kappa)
.

Definition 4.20
.

Let
\Gamma_{\lambda}
be the set of
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentences enumerated below.

(S1)
λ

\ulcorner\forall i\ \forall n\ \forall\alpha\ \forall\beta\ ((E(\ulcorner\dot{F}_{i}(n)=\alpha\urcorner)\wedge E(\ulcorner\dot{F}_{i}(n)=\beta\urcorner))\implies\alpha=\beta)\urcorner
,

(S2)
λ

​
\begin{aligned} \ulcorner&\forall i\ \forall\alpha\ \forall m\ \forall\gamma\ \exists n\ \exists\beta\\
&((E(\ulcorner\dot{F}_{i}(m)=\gamma\urcorner)\vee E(\ulcorner\neg\dot{F}_{i}(m)=\gamma\urcorner))\wedge\alpha\in i\\
&\implies(\alpha\in\beta\wedge E(\ulcorner\dot{F}_{i}(n)=\beta\urcorner)))\urcorner,\end{aligned}

(S3)
λ

​
\begin{aligned} \ulcorner&\forall\delta\ \forall\lambda_{0}\ \forall\lambda_{1}\ \forall x\ \forall y\\
&((E(\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda_{0}}\urcorner)\wedge E(\ulcorner y\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda_{1}}\urcorner))\implies\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{1})\urcorner,\end{aligned}

(S4)
λ

​
\begin{aligned} \ulcorner&\forall\delta_{0}\ \forall\delta_{1}\ \forall\lambda_{0}\ \forall\lambda_{1}\ \forall x\ \forall y\\
&((E(\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta_{0},\lambda_{0}}\urcorner)\wedge E(\ulcorner y\in\dot{X}_{\delta_{1},\lambda_{1}}\urcorner)\wedge\delta_{0}\in\delta_{1})\\
&\implies\lambda_{0}\in\lambda_{1})\urcorner\end{aligned}

(S5)
λ

\ulcorner\forall\delta\ \forall\lambda^{\prime}\ \forall\delta^{\prime}\in\delta\ \forall x\ (E(\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner)\implies E(\ulcorner\delta^{\prime}\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner))\urcorner
,

(S6)
λ

\ulcorner\forall\delta\ \forall\lambda^{\prime}\ \forall\delta^{\prime}\in\omega_{1}\ \forall x\ ((E(\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner)\wedge\delta\in\delta^{\prime})\implies\neg E(\ulcorner\delta^{\prime}\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner))\urcorner
,

(S7)
λ

​
\begin{aligned} \ulcorner&\forall\delta\ \forall\lambda^{\prime}\ \forall\phi\ \forall\bar{s}\ \forall x\ \exists r\\
&((E(\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner)\ \wedge\ \\
&(\phi,\bar{s})\in Ef_{1}^{\lambda}(\lambda^{\prime})\ \wedge\\
&\forall n\in dom(\bar{s})\ (E(\ulcorner\bar{s}(n)\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner)))\\
&\implies(E(\ulcorner r\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner)\wedge(\phi,\bar{s},r)\in Ef_{0}^{\lambda}(\lambda^{\prime})))\urcorner,\end{aligned}

(S8)
λ

​
\begin{aligned} \ulcorner&\forall\delta\ \forall\lambda^{\prime}\ \forall\phi\ \forall\bar{s}\ \forall x\ \exists p\in\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\\
&((E(\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner)\ \wedge\\
&(\phi,\bar{s})\in Df^{\lambda}(\lambda^{\prime})\ \wedge\\
&\forall n\in dom(\bar{s})\ (E(\ulcorner\bar{s}(n)\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner)))\\
&\implies((\phi,\bar{s},p)\in Ef_{0}^{\lambda}(\lambda^{\prime})\ \wedge\\
&E(\ulcorner p\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\urcorner)\ \wedge\\
&\forall e\ ((e\in p\wedge\text{``}\emptyset\neq p\text{ and }p\text{ is Dedekind-finite''})\implies E(e))))\urcorner.\end{aligned}

In Definition
4.20
, we give a list of constraints on the
\dot{F}_{i}
’s and the
\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}
’s, that are meant to dictate how the objects interpreting them behave. To be more formal, let
\Sigma
interpret the unary relation symbol
\ulcorner E\urcorner
occurring in
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
(formulas). Here, we are using the term “interpret” in the conventional model-theoretic sense. Also, let

•

F_{i}:=F_{i}(\Sigma)
, and

•

X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}:=X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}(\Sigma)
,

as
i
,
\delta
and
\lambda^{\prime}
range over their appropriate domains. Then

•

(S1)λ
and
(S3)λ
mean to say that the
F_{i}
’s and the set

\{(\delta,\lambda^{\prime}):X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\}

are functions,

•

(S2)λ
means to say that the image of each
F_{i}
is cofinal in
i
,

•

(S4)λ
means to say that the function

\{(\delta,\lambda^{\prime}):X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\}

is strictly increasing,

•

(S5)λ
and
(S6)λ
mean to say that
X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\cap\omega_{1}=\delta
whenever
X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}
is non-empty,

•

(S7)λ
means to tell us that if
X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}
is non-empty, then
(X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}};\in,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}},A_{\lambda^{\prime}})
is an elementary submodel of
(Q_{\lambda^{\prime}};\in,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}},A_{\lambda^{\prime}})
, and

•

(S8)λ
means to tell us that if
X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}
is non-empty, then for every dense subset
D
of
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
definable over
(Q_{\lambda^{\prime}};\in,\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}},A_{\lambda^{\prime}})
with parameters from
X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}
,

[\Sigma]^{<\omega}\cap X_{\delta,\lambda^{\prime}}\cap D\neq\emptyset.

Now we can define
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}:=(P_{\lambda},\leq_{\lambda})
, where

\displaystyle P_{\lambda}:=\

\displaystyle\{p\in[\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda}]^{<\omega}:\ \Vdash_{Col(\omega,|H(\kappa)|)}\exists\Sigma\ (``\Sigma\ \Gamma_{\lambda}(\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }p")\},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\leq_{\lambda}\ :=\

\displaystyle\{(p,q)\in P_{\lambda}\times P_{\lambda}:q\subset p\}.

We let
\mathbb{P}
denote
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda_{f}}
.

Lemma 4.21
.

|\mathbb{P}|\leq\lambda_{f}
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the observation that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\subset\

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L}]^{<\omega}\subset H(\lambda_{f})\text{, and}

\displaystyle|H(\lambda_{f})|=\lambda_{f}\text{.}\qed

By Proposition
3.54
and the lemma below,
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P})
is good for
\mathcal{L}
. Obviously,
\mathbb{P}
is definable in the language associated with
\mathfrak{A}
because
\mathbb{P}\in H(\kappa)
.

Lemma 4.22
.

For all
\lambda\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}
,
\emptyset\in P_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

Let
g
be
Col(\omega,|H(\kappa)|)
-generic over
W
. In
W[g]
, for every
i\in R
, choose a cofinal map from
\omega
into
i
and call it
F_{i}
. For every
\delta<\omega_{1}
and every
\lambda\in C
, let
X_{\delta,\lambda}
be the empty set. Then

\mathfrak{C}:=\langle\langle F_{i}:i\in R\rangle,\langle X_{\delta,\lambda}:\delta<\omega_{1},\lambda\in C\rangle\rangle

interprets
\mathcal{L}
and
\Sigma(\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda})

\Gamma_{\lambda}(\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
\emptyset
for all
\lambda\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}
.
∎

Using a argument similar to that in the proof of Lemma
4.22
, we get the following.

Lemma 4.23
.

If

•

\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}
,

•

\lambda_{0}\leq\lambda_{1}
, and

•

\Sigma

\Gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda_{0}},\mathfrak{A})
-certify
p
,

then there is
\Sigma^{\prime}\supset\Sigma
for which
\Sigma^{\prime}

\Gamma_{\lambda_{1}}(\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda_{1}},\mathfrak{A})
-certify
p
.

It can be gleaned from Lemma
4.23
and the definition of the
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}
’s that

(P1)

P_{\lambda_{0}}=P_{\lambda_{1}}\cap Q_{\lambda_{0}}
whenever
\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}
and
\lambda_{0}\leq\lambda_{1}
, and

(P2)

P_{\lambda}=\bigcup\{P_{\lambda^{\prime}}:\lambda^{\prime}\in C\cap\lambda\}
whenever
\lambda\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}
and
sup(\lambda\cap C)=\lambda
.

Lemma 4.24
.

Let

•

\lambda^{\prime}\in C\cup\{\lambda_{f}\}
, and

•

g
be a
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
-
\Sigma_{1}
-generic filter over
W
.

Then
\bigcup g\ \Gamma_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }\emptyset
.

Proof.

We apply Lemma
3.56
with

•

\mathfrak{A}
,
\mathcal{L}
and
g
as defined or given above,

•

P_{\lambda^{\prime}}
in place of
P
,

•

\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
in place of
\mathbb{P}
,

•

\Gamma_{\lambda^{\prime}}
in place of
\Gamma
,

•

|H(\kappa)|
in place of
\lambda
,

•

[\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda^{\prime}}]^{<\omega}
in place of
B
,

•

W
in place of
V
, and

•

W[g]
in place of
W
,

noting that

•

|trcl(\mathfrak{A})|\leq|H(\kappa)|
,

•

[\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda^{\prime}}]^{<\omega}
is closed under finite extensions,

•

the definition of
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
in relation to the other parameters is faithful to the hypothesis of Lemma
3.56
,

•

g
satisfies the hypothesis of Lemma
3.56
with respect to the other parameters, and

•

\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
being non-empty (per Lemma
4.22
) implies there is
\Sigma
in some weak outer model
W^{\prime}
of
W
such that
\Sigma

\Gamma_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda^{\prime}},\mathfrak{A})
-certifies
\emptyset
,

to arrive at
\bigcup g\ \Gamma_{\lambda^{\prime}}(\mathcal{L}\cap Q_{\lambda},\mathfrak{A})\text{-certifies }\emptyset
.
∎

The proof of Lemma
4.24
serves as an instructive example of the utility of Lemma
3.56
. We shall omit details in subsequent applications of Lemma
3.56
, wherever the use cases are deemed similarly straightforward.

Lemma 4.25
.

\mathbb{P}
fulfils
(1’)
of Lemma
4.12
.

Proof.

For any
\mathbb{P}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
-generic filter
g
over
W
,
\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\Gamma_{\lambda_{f}}
by Lemma
4.24
. In particular,
\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\text{(S2)}_{\lambda_{f}}
. That
\mathbb{P}
fulfils
(1’)
of Lemma
4.12
follows immediately.
∎

Definition 4.26
.

Let

•

S(\omega_{1})
denote the set of all stationary subsets of
\omega_{1}
, and

•

U(C,\lambda_{f})
denote the set of all subsets of
C
unbounded in
\lambda_{f}
.

Check that both
S(\omega_{1})
and
U(C,\lambda_{f})
are members of
H(\kappa)
.

Lemma 4.27
.

The
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}
-
\Pi_{2}
sentence

\displaystyle\ulcorner\forall x\in H(\lambda_{f})\ \forall S\in S(\omega_{1})\ \forall U\in U(C,\lambda_{f})\ \exists\mu\in S\ \exists\nu\in U\ (E(\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\mu,\nu}\urcorner))\urcorner

(4.2)

is
(\mathfrak{A},\mathbb{P},\mathcal{L})
-universal.

Proof.

Fix arbitrary

•

p\in\mathbb{P}
,

•

x\in H(\lambda_{f})
,

•

S\in S(\omega_{1})
, and

•

U\in U(C,\lambda_{f})
.

By Lemma
3.51
, it suffices to show that there are

•

\mu\in S
, and

•

\nu\in U

for which
p\cup\{\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\mu,\nu}\urcorner\}\in\mathbb{P}
.

To that end, let
\nu\in U
be such that
x\in Q_{\nu}
and
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\nu}
. This is possible by
(P2)
. Choose
g\times f
a
\mathbb{P}_{\nu}\times Col(\omega,\nu)
-generic filter over
W
with
p\in g
, so that
g\in W[g\times f]
is a
\mathbb{P}_{\nu}
-generic filter over
W
and
|\nu|^{W[g\times f]}=\omega
. By Lemma
4.24
,
\bigcup g\models^{*}_{\mathfrak{A}}\Gamma_{\nu}
.

Since
\mathrm{NS}_{\omega_{1}}
is precipitous in
W
,
(W;\in,(\mathrm{NS}_{\omega_{1}})^{W})
is generically iterable in
W[g\times f]
. Consider a one-step iteration

\mathfrak{I}_{1}=\langle(W;\in,(\mathrm{NS}_{\omega_{1}})^{W}),(W_{1};\in,I_{1})\rangle

in
W[g\times f]
, where
(W_{1};\in,I_{1})
is the generic ultrapower of
(W;\in,(\mathrm{NS}_{\omega_{1}})^{W})
via a
W
-generic ultrafilter on
(\mathrm{NS}_{\omega_{1}})^{W}
containing
S
. Extend
\mathfrak{I}_{1}
to a generic iteration
\mathfrak{I}
of length
\omega_{1}^{W[g\times f]}+1
in
W[g\times f]
. Said iteration gives rise to a generic ultrapower map
j:W\longrightarrow M
, where
M
, an inner model of
W[g\times f]
, is the final iterate of
\mathfrak{I}
. Moreover,

•

crit(j)=\omega_{1}^{W}\in j(S)
, and

•

j(\omega_{1}^{W})=\omega_{1}^{W[g\times f]}
.

Let

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=j"(\bigcup g)\cup\{\ulcorner j(y)\in\dot{X}_{\omega_{1}^{W}\!\!,j(\nu)}\urcorner:y\in Q_{\nu}\}\subset j(\mathcal{L}),

\displaystyle e

\displaystyle\text{ be }Col(\omega,j(\lambda_{f}))\text{-generic over }W[g\times f]\text{, and}

\displaystyle W^{*}

\displaystyle:=W[g\times f][e].

Working in
W^{*}
, define

\mathfrak{C}:=\langle\langle F_{i}:i\in j(R)\rangle,\langle X_{\delta,\lambda}:\delta<\omega_{1}^{M},\lambda\in j(C)\rangle\rangle

as follows:

•

F_{i}:=F_{i}(\Sigma^{\prime})
whenever
\dot{F}_{i}
occurs in
\Sigma^{\prime}
,

•

F_{i}
is some (any) strictly increasing cofinal function from
\omega
into
i
whenever
\dot{F}_{i}
does not occur in
\Sigma^{\prime}
, and

•

x\in X_{\delta,\lambda}
iff
\ulcorner x\in\dot{X}_{\delta,\lambda}\urcorner\in\Sigma^{\prime}
.

Then
\mathfrak{C}
interprets
j(\mathcal{L})
, and
\Sigma^{*}:=\Sigma(\mathfrak{C},j(\mathcal{L}))
is
j(\mathcal{L})
-nice, noting Remark
4.19
. Obviously
j"\bigcup g\subset\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma^{*}
, so
j(p)=j"p\subset\Sigma^{*}
. By the definition of
\Sigma^{\prime}
, we too have

q^{*}:=j(p)\cup\{\ulcorner j(x)\in\dot{X}_{\omega_{1}^{W}\!\!,j(\nu)}\urcorner\}\subset\Sigma^{*}.

In order to conclude that
\Sigma^{*}

j(\Gamma_{\lambda_{f}})(j(\mathcal{L}),j(\mathfrak{A}))
-certifies
q^{*}
